- **Vulkan runs on the 7T (2026-11-13, observed): upstream Turnip over RedwoodGPU, no kernel change.**
  - Build: `.github/workflows/vulkan.yml` (macos-15, Xcode 16.4 / iOS 18.5 SDK, target iOS 16.0). Mesa 85fda3b3 + `vulkan/mesa-patches` 0001-0005 (libdrm lookup on darwin for the msm KMD; CTR_EL0 constant 0x8444c004 because an EL0 `mrs ctr_el0` is SIGILL on the phone while `dc cvac/civac` work; BO maps through sweetdrm; no pthread_condattr_setclock; `-undefined dynamic_lookup` on libvulkan_freedreno only, for Mesa's weak optional entrypoints, 5536 left NULL). `vulkan/sweetdrm/sweetdrm.c` = the libdrm calls (header from libdrm-2.4.124), syncobjs in userspace. CI fails if anything non-weak is left to dynamic lookup.
  - Run 43387059461, on the phone in `/var/local/vk` (`vktest /var/local/vk/libvulkan_freedreno.dylib`, TU_DEBUG=startup): "Turnip Adreno (TM) 640, Vulkan 1.3.354", device id 0x6040001; memory types 0x7 (coherent) and 0xb (cached); instance 2.7 ms, device 20.8 ms; **vkCmdFillBuffer of 1 MiB + fence: 0.52 ms first, then 0.31 ms; 5 rounds, 0 of 262144 words wrong.**
  - Found in that run: sweetdrm waited with CLOCK_MONOTONIC deadlines, but RedwoodGPU's msm_timespec is mach_continuous_time (62 s apart here), so kernel waits ended at once ("over before it began") and sweetdrm polled. Fixed to convert deadlines (run 43387626368, not yet on the phone).
  - Clock fix checked (run 43387626368): fill test still 0 wrong, 0.34 ms a round, no "over before it began" in dmesg.
  - **First shader (run 43387867851, `vulkan/vktest/vkcompute.c` + `compute.comp`):** compute pipeline built on the phone (SPIR-V -> NIR -> ir3) in **5.4 ms**; out[i] = in[i]*3 + i*k over 4M words (16 MiB in, 16 MiB out, output in the cached memory type, read after vkInvalidateMappedMemoryRanges): **3.1-3.4 ms per dispatch + fence (~10.7 GB/s), 0 wrong in 5 rounds**, nothing in dmesg.
  - Next: a draw; then measure submit cost (Turnip lists every BO each submit, RedwoodGPU pushes every mapped one).
- **Vulkan/Metal plan, my own build (2026-11-13).** Not running yet: the 7T has never had Vulkan (no Turnip on the phone, nothing in git). I copy no code from ios-flame/redfin/UniXNU (their work is reference only); upstream Mesa and steelbrain/metal2vulkan are the foundations.
  - Seen: my `libOSMesa.8.dylib` (freedreno GL) already uses Mesa's **msm** backend, routed to RedwoodGPU (imports IOServiceOpen/IOConnectCallStructMethod/IOConnectMapMemory64; string "RedwoodGPU"). RedwoodGPU's selectors are the DRM ioctl numbers: 0x09 GEM_CLOSE, 0x40 GET_PARAM, 0x41 SET_PARAM, 0x42 GEM_NEW, 0x43 GEM_INFO, 0x44/0x45 CPU_PREP/FINI, 0x46 GEM_SUBMIT, 0x47 WAIT_FENCE, 0x48 MADVISE, 0x4a-0x4c SUBMITQUEUE NEW/CLOSE/QUERY.
  - Turnip's msm backend (Mesa 85fda3b3, `tu_knl_drm_msm.cc`) uses the same calls plus: drmGetVersion (name "msm"), DRM syncobjs (create/destroy/wait/reset/signal, timeline cap; `MSM_SUBMIT_SYNCOBJ_IN/OUT` in submit), prime fd import/export (external memory only), `mmap(fd, GET_OFFSET)` for CPU maps. RedwoodGPU has no VA_START/VA_SIZE param, so Turnip takes the kernel-assigned IOVA path (no SET_IOVA, no VM_BIND).
  - Plan: (1) my own `libsweetdrm`: the libdrm calls Turnip uses, over RedwoodGPU's IOKit client; **syncobjs in userspace** (a syncobj = a submit fence of the one ring; in-waits on GPU work are already ordered by the single ring, CPU-signalled ones are waited for before submit; out-syncobjs take the returned fence); (2) small Turnip patches of mine: device open without /dev/dri, BO map through IOConnectMapMemory64, exported vkGetInstanceProcAddr (no loader on iOS), no EL0 CTR_EL0 read; (3) macOS CI build; (4) a vkcube-style test on the phone; (5) then my own Metal layer over metal2vulkan's runtime, built for few copies (flame's Metal UI: 19-26 fps vs my GL 60).

    - SpringBoard: ~24 FrontBoard worker threads wait on a lock held by thread 0xf1b, which is in a sync XPC call to runningboardd (waits on its thread 0xe0c). Main thread idle.
    - runningboardd 0xe0c: `-[RBProcessManager executeLaunchRequest:withError:]` -> `-[RBLaunchManager executeLaunchRequest:existingProcess:requestIdentity:withError:]` -> `-[RBLaunchdJobManager createAndLaunchWithIdentity:context:error:]` -> `-[OSLaunchdJob start:]` (AppServerSupport) -> blocked in mach_msg, no turnstile owner shown.
    - launchd: idle in kevent (threads named "user/501/com.apple.wifip2pd throttled after exit()"); no xpcproxy waiting, no U/D-state process; `launchctl print user/501` has UIKitApplication jobs for apps prelaunched at boot (Spotlight, weather, findmy, DocumentsApp, Passbook, MobileStore, calculator; all [rb-legacy]) but none for Safari/Settings.
    - So runningboardd's synchronous job start to launchd never returns and every later app launch queues behind it. Which job it is starting and why launchd never answers: not found yet (guess: one of the boot-time background app launches). Happened ~2 min after boot (load 50). Unrelated to 0199's property change (guess, but it touches only a display property; I saw it before 0199). Next time: before rebooting, `launchctl print user/501` + `launchctl print system` for jobs in "spawn scheduled"/"running" without pid, and runningboardd's oslog lines for "Executing launch request".

### >>> 2026-11-12: CAMERA bring-up (unattended run) <<<
- **Goal of step 1/2:** power the rear main sensor (live DT `qcom,cam-sensor@0`), give it MCLK, read its chip ID over the CCI. No frames yet (CSIPHY/CSID/IFE later), no iOS side yet.
- **Facts (live DT + Linux sources downloaded to scratchpad linux-src/: camcc-sm8150.c, gcc-sm8150.c, pinctrl-sm8150.c, i2c-qcom-cci.c):**
  - Sensor@0: CCI device 0 = `qcom,cci@ac4a000` (irq 0x1cc), **master 1** (TLMM gpio19 SDA1 / gpio20 SCL1, NORTH tile, function 1 cci_i2c; master 0 = gpio17/18). MCLK1 on gpio14 (NORTH, function 1 cam_mclk), 19.2 MHz. Reset gpio30 (EAST). Analog switches gpio11 CAM_VANA_0 (NORTH), gpio29 CAM_VANA2_0 (EAST). CAM_PVDD = PM8150L (SID 4) GPIO1; its DT "active" state is output-low (odd for an enable: check).
  - Supplies: cam_vio = PM8150L L1 = RPMh **ldoc1** (compatible rpmh-xob-regulator: on/off only, 1.8 V); cam_vana = PM8150L BOB = **bobc1** (VRM, pmic5-bob, init 3.312 V, modes pass/auto/pwm); cam_vdig = PM8009 (SPMI SID 0xa) L1 = **ldof1** (VRM, 1.104 V); cam_clk = camcc **TITAN_TOP_GDSC** (camcc+0xc1bc, POLL_CFG_GDSCR, en_rest/en_few wait 2, clk_dis wait 0xf).
  - Camera clock controller 0xad00000: MCLK1 RCG 0x5024 (parent map 1: XO = src 0; 19.2 MHz = XO /1) + branch 0x503c; CCI0 RCG 0xc108 + branch 0xc120 (19.2 MHz = XO; vendor DT runs 37.5 MHz from PLL0); CCI1 0xc124/0xc13c; cpas_ahb 0xc168; camnoc_axi 0xc18c (RCG 0xc170); core_ahb 0xc1c8; gdsc_clk 0xc1e4 (Linux keeps it on); slow_ahb RCG 0x7058. GCC: camera_ahb 0xb008 and camera_xo 0xb044 are CLK_IS_CRITICAL in Linux; hf_axi 0xb030, sf_axi 0xb034.
  - TLMM tiles (mainline sm8150.dtsi): west 0x03100000, east 0x03500000, north 0x03900000, south 0x03d00000; gpio n cfg at tile + 0x1000*n (pull [1:0], func [5:2], drive [8:6], oe bit 9), io at +4.
  - CCI (Linux i2c-qcom-cci.c, v2: 2 masters, queues 64/16 words): HW_VERSION 0, RESET_CMD 4 (mask 0x0f73f3f7), QUEUE_START 8, master m SCL_CTL 0x100+0x100m, SDA_CTL_0/1/2, MISC_CTL 0x110 (bit 8 SCL stretch), READ_DATA 0x118, READ_BUF_LEVEL 0x11c; queue (m,n) EXEC_WORD_CNT 0x300+0x200m+0x100n, CUR_WORD_CNT +4, LOAD_DATA +0x10; IRQ MASK 0xc04, CLEAR 0xc08, STATUS 0xc0c (M1 RD_DONE bit 12, M1 Q0/Q1 REPORT 16/20, RST_DONE 24, M1 Q0/Q1 NACK 29/30, M1 errors 0x60ee6000), GLOBAL_CLEAR 0xc00. Words: SET_PARAM 1 | addr7<<4; WRITE 9 | len<<4 then data bytes packed 4 per word; READ 10 | len<<4; REPORT 8 | bit 8. Read data: first byte of READ_DATA is to be skipped. 19.2 MHz standard-mode timing: thigh 78 tlow 114 tsu_sto 28 tsu_sta 28 thd_dat 10 thd_sta 77 tbuf 118 trdhld 6 tsp 1.
  - My helpers: `sweet_rpmh_vreg_enable(res, mV)` (VRM only, allowlist; writes voltage@0, mode 7@8, enable 1@4), `sweet_spmi_read(sid, addr)` (allowlist), no SPMI write except the PON reason.
- **Safety rule while unattended:** never read a block whose clock may be off (a stall = hung phone = session over); step 1 is read-only.

Defaults: frame_length_lines 0x17b0 (6064), line_length_pck 0x24e0 (9440); 0x0300.. = 00 05 00 02 00 02 01 68 (vt_pix_clk_div 5, vt_sys_clk_div 2, pre_pll_clk_div 2, pll_multiplier 0x168, from my 19.2 MHz MCLK); data format 0x0112 = 0x0a0a (RAW10); csi_lane_mode 0x0114 = 0x02 (3 lanes). Left in standby.
- **STOPPED HERE ON PURPOSE (unattended):** real sensor frames into memory is the first step that can corrupt RAM: the IFE's SMMU bank is an identity map of 0x80000000-0xffffffff (all of RAM below 4 GiB), and a full IMX586 frame is ~60 MB; a wrong WM bound = the IFE overwriting kernel memory with the root fs mounted read-write. **Next (next time at the phone):**
  1. a bounded SMMU mapping for the IFE (a context bank whose tables map only the capture buffer; an overrun then faults instead of writing RAM);
  2. CSIPHY1 (`qcom,csiphy@ac66000`, csiphy-sd-index 1) D-PHY setup (Linux camss-csiphy-3ph-1-0.c, sdm845 tables; downloaded to scratchpad linux-src/) + csiphy1 / csi1phytimer clocks; CSID0 RX from PHY 1 with the sensor's lane count (default 3; 4 via 0x0114 = 3);
  3. a small sensor window (SMIA/CCS registers 0x0344-0x034f crop + output size, or the vendor mode table) and the WM's frame/line bounds (width/height cfg, frame increment) for that size;
  4. a sysctl to read the buffer to user space, then convert RAW10 Bayer to an image (dark room: black is OK if it is really the camera).
  5. The vendor sensor tables (TWRP pull) for proper modes; then the iOS side (AVFoundation expects Apple's ISP driver: the big part).

Next camera step: a parser for this format (tools/), pick the module by the EEPROM id, then use its lane assignment + a small mode for the first real frame.
  - Earlier backups (Oct 18) already had persist.img: camera calibration/OTP files (`/camera/*`, `/dual_camera_calibration/*`, incl. imx586_s5k3m5 OTP text), not register tables.
- **Vendor sensor tables DECODED (2026-11-12): `tools/chromatix_regs.py MODULE.bin [--all]`.** Format reversed from com.qti.sensormodule.semco_imx586.bin (no public parser/XML found; Qualcomm keeps imx586_sensor.xml in the proprietary chi-cdk): schema of 0x48-byte records from 0x310 (`ff ff ff ff 0 | u64 offset | u64 size | u64 id | name[40]`) to ~0xb6b48, then a 64-bit value stream in schema order; a register write is `[id] ADDR COUNT [id+1] ADDRTYPE DATATYPE OP X [id+2] ..` and each table (run of writes) is followed by its own pool of `(value, 0)` pairs. Checks: init starts 0136=13 0137=33 (19.2 MHz EXCK), the sensor-id block reads slave 0x34, reg 0x16 = 0x586.
  - semco and ofilm imx586 files: **identical init/mode tables** (only one small EEPROM table differs): the module maker does not matter for setup. Decoded table text: `backup/imx586_regs.txt` (git-ignored, derived from vendor data).
  - 41 tables: 0-9 modes, 10/11 stream on/off (0100), 12/13 group hold (0104), 15 = **init (1710 writes)**, 16-19 partial/aux, 20 test pattern 0600, 21-40 EEPROM/OTP. Modes (all RAW10, 3 lanes): 0/1/2/9 4000x3000 2x2 binned, 3 2000x1128, **4 1296x736 (4x4 binned, line 4496, frame 796, ~30 fps)**, 5 2000x1500, 6/7 4000x3000 unbinned, 8 8000x6000. Mode 4 op PLL 19.2/12*1280 = 2048 MHz, op_sys_div 2 -> ~1 Gbps/lane.
  - **Verified on the sensor (0204 kernel, `camio.py table /var/root/imx586_regs.txt 15 4`, 1809 writes in 0.8 s): output 0x0510 x 0x02e0 = 1296x736, lanes 0x02, EXCK 0x1333; stream on -> frame_count 35 -> 69 in ~1 s = ~30 fps.**

- **0207** (commit 894443b, arm64 CI 43295564259, kernel 4c4defca): `kern.sweetcamstop=N` makes =4 stop after stage N (1 PLLs/clocks, 2 buffer, 3 SMMU window, 4 fill, 5 CSIPHY1, 6 IFE0, 7 CSID0). **Booted: stage 1 fine (PLLs locked, every clock on); stage 2 (the 2 MiB buffer: kmem_alloc_contig with pnum_mask = atop(2 MiB) - 1) FROZE the phone again.** So the freeze is the physically aligned contig allocation, nothing in the camera hardware.

- **0209** (commit 5a85e78, arm64 CI 43299836008, kernel ac5b213b): no ml_io_map; the buffer at its cached kernel address with flush_dcache64 (clean+invalidate). **Booted: ALL arm stages pass, no freeze** (so the 2 MiB uncached ml_io_map was the freeze). Buffer 0xc7800000, window bank 53, CSIPHY hw version 0x10000000. Stream 1 s (frame_count 40) then =5: CSIPHY status 04 00 80 00 00, **CSID top/rx/rdi0 all 0, byte counters 0, IFE irq 0, buffer unchanged**; SMMU bank 53 FSR 0x400 FAR 0x150881e7ebdfe (meaning unclear: no IFE writes happened).
  - **Cause: the IMX586 module is MIPI C-PHY.** The decoded init table writes 0x0111 = 3 (CSI signalling mode: 2 D-PHY, 3 C-PHY): 3 trios (hence "3 lanes"); the SoC PHY is the 3-phase CSIPHY v1.1. 0205 configured D-PHY. Mainline camss has no C-PHY; Qualcomm's downstream driver in the OnePlus SM8150 kernel source (github OnePlusOSS/android_kernel_oneplus_sm8150, branch oneplus/SM8150_R_10.0, drivers/media/platform/msm/camera/cam_sensor_module/cam_csiphy/: cam_csiphy_core.c + include/cam_csiphy_1_1_hwreg.h; copies in scratchpad linux-src/ds/) has it.
- **0210** (commit 3a94751): CSIPHY1 3-phase per downstream cam_csiphy_config_dev: 0x814 = 0x2a (bit 2i+1 per trio), 0x818 = 1, 0x81c = 2, 0x800 = 1 / 1 ms / 0, csiphy_3ph_v1_1_reg for trios 0..2 (43 writes each, settle 0x12), csiphy_irq_reg_1_1; CSID RX CFG0 bit 24 (PHY type C-PHY). `sweetcamdphy=1` = old D-PHY. **Booted (arm64 CI 43302078692, kernel de630f2b): C-PHY WORKS.** Stream 1 s then =5: CSIPHY status 00 20 18 00 04; **CSID rx 0x00400077, rdi0 0x00001ff8, byte counters 0xc43c95aa / 0xc43bf9a4; IFE irq0 0x08000220, irq1 0x4, bus irq 0x4**: frames reached the IFE and it wrote. But SMMU window bank 53 FSR 0x402 (translation fault) with **FAR 0xc7800000 = the buffer's own base**: the window's tables were empty -> nothing landed (buffer unchanged; nothing written elsewhere either).
  - Cause: the window tables were written through a write-combining second mapping while KMA_ZERO's zeroes sat dirty in the cache under the cached address; their later eviction wiped the entries.
- **0211** (commit f21c79e, kernel efcaba2e, `shim/out/shim-boot-0211-7g-bt-ddr.img`): tables written through the cached kva and flush_dcache64'd. **Booted: SENSOR DATA REACHES DDR, but only 5-6 bursts per run.** Stream 1 s then =5: CSID rx 0x00400077 (SOT/EOT trios 0-2, bit 22 unmapped VC/DT = probably embedded/PDAF data, no CRC/ECC errors), rdi0 0x1ff8 (same as the working 0204 TPG run), IFE irq0 0x08000220 (RDI0 SOF + RUP) irq1 0x4, bus irq 0x4/0/0 (TPG had 0x4/**0x1**/0 = WM0 done), **WM0 status0/1 = 0** (TPG: status0 = buffer address). SMMU FSR 0x400 = only the FORMAT field (bits 10:9), **no fault**; FAR is stale (the 'write outside the buffer' text in the report is wrong). Buffer: first **1536 bytes** = RAW10 at black level (`10 0f 0f 10 3c ...`, 5-byte groups, 0x40 pedestal), rest still 0xa5. Re-armed in the same boot (works) with the sensor's test pattern (0x0600/0x0601 = 2): first **1280 bytes all 0x00** (no pedestal: really the sensor's data), rest 0xa5. Sensor ~30 fps (0x0005 0x0e -> 0x33 in ~1.2 s). Lead: I never program WM0 framedrop/irq-subsample (0x2248..0x2254, downstream writes pattern 1 period 0) and leave frame_inc 0 with no new image address per frame (downstream RDI: frame_inc = stride*height, fresh address before every RUP). Frame dumps: scratchpad only (frame0211.raw, frame2.raw).
- **0212** (commit after fd59128, arm64 CI 43312671671 / intel 43312706721): `kern.sweetmmio` allowlist + Titan camera block 0x0ac00000 (0x140000, incl. camcc) and apps SMMU 0x15000000 (0x100000). New `tools/camreg.py` (phone: /var/root/camreg.py): `rd`/`wr` (ife+OFF, csid+OFF, phy+, cc+, smmu+), dumps `ife`/`wm`/`csid`, `rewm BUFPA [FRAME_INC]` = WM0 the downstream way. Arm (=4) before touching camera registers (clocks/GDSC off = fault).

Fix from userland after =4: `camreg.py wr cc+c174 207; camreg.py wr cc+c170 1` (PLL0 even 600 / 4 = **150 MHz**; downstream table 19.2/150/266/320/400/480). Then: bus irq1 0x1 (WM0 done), WM0 status0 = buffer, **1201152 bytes = 736 rows x 1632** (WM pads each 1620-byte RAW10 line to 16 bytes: `raw10png.py IN OUT 1296 736 1632`). - **What the zip is:** extracted to `C:\Users\Felix\Documents\redfin-extract\ios-redfin-main` (outside the repo, untrusted, nothing in it was run). Its README describes ios-redfin (Pixel 5, SM7250/A620), but the zip's `main` branch is the inherited **ios-flame base = Pixel 4, SM8150, Adreno 640: my SoC and GPU**, iOS 16.7.16, xnu-8796 + ios-sweet 0001-0081, flame patches 0082-0124. Redfin's own patches (0900-1030: 90 Hz, DVFS governor, direct scanout) are on branches that are not in the zip. HackiOS (`~/Downloads/hakios-sm8150-main`) has no Metal code.
- **Their GPU stack is not mine:** their own A640 core (`bsd/dev/sweet_a6xx.c`) + `SweetAccelerator` (IOAcceleratorES); CA renders through **Metal** (v1: host-made pipeline templates; v2: metal2vulkan + Turnip). Ours: HackiOS RedwoodGPU + Mesa freedreno GL (libredwood-gl/OSMesa) under CA's OGL path. So their kernel GPU code does not drop in; their findings and their userland Metal bundle do.
- **Their fixes vs mine (observed = measured or read in my files; guesses marked):**
  - **0086 CPU clock** (ABL left their A76s at 710/825 MHz): my shim already does it (`sweetcpufreq=1`, shim/gpu.c cpu_boost, in the current line). **Measured on the phone with `tools/cpuhz.c`: A76 ~2415 MHz, prime ~2790 MHz.** Nothing to do. L3 clock (OSM `osm_l3_base` 0x18321000) is raised by neither project: unknown.
  - **0121 vsync period: applies, done as 0190.** My `vsyncFired` sent a3 = 0. Checked in my QuartzCore 16.5 (`QuartzCore` in the repo root, `vsync_callback` 0x1882b1800: `add x22, x21, x22` -> `CATimeWithHostTime`): a2 + a3 = next vsync, so CA saw every deadline as past. Flame: swipe test 15.8 -> 21 fps. **0190** sends the period and the tick's scheduled time (`sweetvsyncperiod=0` = old). My home screen is already at 60, so expect the gain in apps/scrolling/animation pacing, not the home swipe (guess).
  - **0105/0106 GPU VA reuse + more page tables: applies, done as 0191.** My window was 768 MiB (the shim's one L2); 11-10 idle already 386 MB / 414 buffers / 58 holes. **0191**: window 0x10000000..0xffffffff (3840 MiB), one lazily made L2 per GiB hung in the shim's empty L1[1..3]; `sweetgpuva=768` = old.
  - **0122 DDR + memory-NoC vote at GPU start** (flame measured: 8-thread CPU bandwidth 13.7 -> 18-19.6 GB/s, present blit 1.65 -> 1.1 ms, backboardd submits ~20 % faster): **my shim has it (`sweetddr=1`, shim/gpu.c:1385) but it was never booted and is not in the current line** (0180-7g-bthook line checked). Boot-line only, 11 bytes: add `sweetddr=1` to the next image. Matters more for me because `sweetgpudirty=0` (current) pushes ~12.8 MB per submission with dc civac, which is bandwidth-bound. Battery cost: DDR held at max (guess: noticeable when idle).
  - **0107 GPU perf vote:** I run `sweetgpufreq=5` (585 MHz). `6` = 675 MHz, only on speed bin 1 (the shim prints the bin on its console).
  - **0115's big lesson, applies fully:** without PPL the physmap maps all managed RAM with cacheable blocks; any WC/NC view of a managed page is a mismatched alias (their Turnip command buffers read back wrong: 15 of 410 dwords; v2test 2496 of 16384 words). Their fix: **GPU buffers from RAM XNU never manages**, zeroed through a WC kernel view, WC CPU maps, **no cache maintenance at all**. That is the real fix for my per-submission push and the 0145/0146 triangles (guess for the triangles: dirty-bit tracking cannot see lines brought in through the cacheable physmap alias or by speculation). Not cheap (plan below), not written.
  - **Their pmap fix** (`pmap_sync_page_attributes_phys`: 32-bit ppnum_t << PAGE_SHIFT): my 10-22 "WC panic" (`phystokv_range: illegal PA 0x78778000` = real 0x1E1DE0000 / 4) is a factor-4 error, not a 4 GB truncation, so probably a different spot (not checked: no local xnu tree). Even fixed, WC on managed pages stays a mismatched alias.
  - Not perf or already covered: 0108 GPU hang recovery (mine: not checked), 0109/0119 async submit/present (I have 0109), 0113 wait for a client's last fence before unmap, 0110-0112 rounded corners, 0104 planar bytes-per-row, 0124 10-bit surfaces as BGRA only for WebContent, 0123 Wi-Fi.
- **Metal through metal2vulkan, what flame got (their notes, 2026-10-22):** `SweetMetal2.bundle` (UniXNU's frontend `NativeBridgeMetalFrontend.mm`, patched for iOS 16) -> metal2vulkan bridge (Rust staticlib, steelbrain/metal2vulkan pin 720ef1ce + UniXNU's 5 patches + their 0100/0101) -> Mesa Turnip (Mesa 85fda3b3 + UniXNU's 8 patches incl. the darwin KMD `tu_knl_darwin.cc`, built for iOS 16) -> kernel "NativeAdreno ABI v1" user client (their 0115, 1029 added lines). Shaders are translated and compiled **on the phone** (~270 CA pipelines cold, 3-30 ms each; cache per process in /var/local/m2v-cache). Correct: lock/home/Settings/Control Center/Spotlight/Clock/Calendar/Notes. Wrong: Today widgets white (chronod excluded), Safari body white, Photos crashed once. **Frame rate: swipe 19-26 fps, GPU busy 29-47 %, 7-9 ms per submit** (redfin README later: home ~60, Settings ~20 fps, GPU-bound).
- **Assessment:**
  - **Not a performance win for the UI.** My GL path does the home screen at 60 fps; flame's Metal path did 19-26 on the same GPU. Turnip + metal2vulkan cost more per frame (shadow copies of CA's frame, pass overhead) than my GL path does today. Keep backboardd on GL.
  - **What it gives:** a real `MTLDevice` (render, compute, blit, depth/stencil, MRT, heaps, fences, MSAA, framebuffer fetch) for the processes listed in a clients file: third-party apps and games that need Metal, MapKit/VectorKit, RenderBox widgets, CoreImage. Plan: backboardd stays GL, apps get Metal (flame's `/var/local/mtl2-clients` with `-NAME` excludes; whether an app's CA behaves with a device while backboardd has none is untested). Expect gaps: whatever metal2vulkan refuses (its REFUSALS.md), cold-compile stalls on first launch, pool memory (60-130 MB per process on flame).
  - **Kernel needs (none of it exists in RedwoodGPU):** (1) an IOAcceleratorES service with `MetalPluginName`/`MetalPluginClassName` (flame 0096/0116; Metal picks the bundle from it); (2) the NativeAdreno ABI v1 user client (open type 0x4147, struct methods via selector 0: Query, GetDeviceInfo, BoAlloc, BoMap at a client-chosen IOVA, BoCpuMap write-combined, BoImport of IOSurface/user pages, Submit of up to 16 IBs wrapped like msm a6xx_submit (CCU invalidate first, fence by CACHE_FLUSH_TS), WaitFence in ns, timestamps, per-connection VA windows); (3) **an unmanaged RAM pool** for those buffers (lower the ADT dram-size, e.g. 7 GiB -> 6 GiB, and give the top GiB to the GPU; check what the shim/ABL put there first); (4) sharing my one ring and SMMU context with RedwoodGPU (flame ran its v1 and v2 clients on one core, every process in one context with its own window; 0191's L1 hookup extends to windows above 4 GiB the same way).
  - **Userland:** flame's `phone/metal2` + `tools/build-turnip-ios.sh` + `tools/build-m2v-bridge.sh` target the same A640 and iOS 16, so their bundle and Turnip should work unchanged if my kernel speaks the same ABI under the same names. Builds need macOS (Xcode, rustup aarch64-apple-ios, meson) or WSL with the theos iPhoneOS16.5 SDK (how my Mesa was built); CI macOS runners are another route.
  - **Effort (guess):** kernel 2-4 days (port 0115's user client onto sweet_gpu.c's ring/submit + the pool + the ADT change), userland 1-2 days to build and bring up (flame went from Turnip running to the home screen through metal2vulkan in about a day once the ABI worked), then open-ended app-compatibility work.

- **2026-11-11: Bluetooth power off/on fixed (hook, verified by toggling in Settings).**
  - **Crash cause (static, bluetoothd 20F66):** the H4 transport table entry (`__DATA,__data` 0x100844218: type 1, name "H4") has an empty slot at +0x60. H4BC/H5/PCIe/APPLEBT fill theirs. The teardown at 0x1000e656c only skips type 0 (USB), then `br` to that slot -> jump to 0 (SIGSEGV pc 0, the 02:48/02:55 crashes). Real iPhones never use plain H4.
  - **Fix:** bthook's constructor finds bluetoothd by image name and fills the slot with a do-nothing function, after checking the entry (type 1, pointer to "H4").
    - `_dyld_get_image_header(0)` is NOT bluetoothd when a library is inserted; its "H4" bytes read as zeros.
    - Off now: "Terminating stack..." -> "bthook: H4 transport +0x60 called" -> STACK_OFF, no crash.
  - **Power-on exit:** after the reconnect, bluetoothd quit within a second. Cause: the hook's packet parser kept a half packet from the old connection ("dropped 0 queued + 4 partial bytes"). Now reset at every TIOCSETA (new connection). On: the same bluetoothd reconnects -> STACK_READY.
  - **Pairings now survive:** "Paired device cache contains 1 device(s)" after restarts. The loss on 11-10 came with the crash.
- **2026-11-11: volume keys fixed (0193) + audio amps answer on I2C.**
  - **Keys:** sweet_keys.c still read the Redmi wiring: volume up from PM6150L SID 4 GPIO 2 (absent on the 7T: never worked), volume down from PON RESIN (never set by the 7T's key). Before 0193, four presses all logged as key 0 (power).
    - Live DT gpio_keys: vol_up = PM8150 (SID 0) GPIO 6, vol_down = GPIO 7, active-low.
    - **0193** (`xnu/patches/0193-op7t-volume-keys.patch`, CI 43139365299, kernel 27e539ed): power = PON 0x810 bit 0, volume up = !0xc510 bit 0, volume down = !0xc610 bit 0 or RESIN. Raw registers are logged on change; `sweetkeys7t=0` gives the old reads.
    - **Image `shim/out/shim-boot-0193-7g-bt-ddr.img`** (the 0192 line): presses log `key=1 (volume up)` on GPIO6 and `key=2 (volume down)` on GPIO7; **confirmed by hand: the volume slider moves.**
  - **Audio amps:** `SWEET_TLMM=1 python3 /var/root/sweetaudio.py status / i2c-init / tfa-id` on the 0193 boot.
    - SE4 = I2C firmware (fw 0x303, 32-bit FIFOs); pins 51/52 muxed to qup4; master at 400 kHz.
    - Reset pins 37/100 read 0 (TFA reset is active-high, so out of reset).
    - **tfa 0x34 (right) and 0x35 (left): register 0x03 = 0x0c74 -> TFA9874** (the low byte is the type: 0x74 = TFA9874, 0x94 would be TFA9894), even though the vendor module is named audio_tfa9894.ko (NXP's driver covers both).
    - Next: TFA9874 init from the container (backup/vendor-audio/tfa98xx.cnt; NXP open tfa98xx tfa2 rules: patch/config/preset for the 9874), MI2S pins 137-142 to function 1, then the data path (AFE port 0x1006 SD1, ASM stream).

  - Sources: NXP's open tfa98xx driver (Goodix-vas/tfa98xx on GitHub): tfa_init.c `tfa9874_specific` (rev 0x0c74 "V1.16" values), tfa_dsp.c `tfaRunStartup`, `tfa_dev_set_state`, `tfa98xx_set_mute_tfa2` (TFA2 mute = AMPE). The TFA9874 is a "probus" device without CoolFlux: speaker protection would run on the host DSP, so plain playback needs no patch or .msg files.
  - **Tools:**
    - `tools/tfacnt.py` has `lists()`: per-device and per-profile register/bitfield writes.

    1. `sysctl -w kern.sweetadsp=1` (stage 6). `apr-state` said ENXIO right after; a minute later it answered AVCS 1.
    2. `tfa-init`: I2CR, keys, the 13 rev writes, 51 device + 6 speaker-profile writes; PWDN 1, AMPE 0.
    3. `mi2s-pins`.
    4. `afe-start 2 32`: clock 3.072 MHz, I2S 32-bit stereo on SD1, port started (all "done"). The container sets 32-bit slots/samples (TDMSLLN/TDMSSIZE 31, TDMNBCK 2); amp 0x34 takes slot 0, 0x35 slot 1.
    5. `tfa-up` (MANSCONF 1, PWDN 0).
  - **Result, both amps:** CLKS 1, PLLS 1 (PLL locked to my BCK), MANSTATE 9 (operating), AMPS 1 / SWS 1, MANALARM 0.
    - The chip set AMPE 1 by itself on entering operating (the amp ran on the DSP's silence; no stream is open).
    - `tfa-mute` (AMPE 0) holds: SWS 0, still MANSTATE 9 with clocks locked.
    - Reg 0x10 reads 0x0016 (OTDS/UVDS bits set) with MANALARM 0: those seem to be OK-polarity flags (guess).
  - **No sound yet: nothing feeds the AFE port.** Next is the data path:
    - an ASM stream (open, shared-memory map, write PCM) -> ADM (copp) -> AFE port 0x1006.
    - It needs memory the ADSP can reach (apps SMMU stream for LPASS, as vayu_audio.c does): a kernel patch.
    - Then a quiet test tone (low amplitude), unmute.
  - **Data-path plan (from HackiOS vayu_audio.c sound=2/4, same SM8150):**
    - The DSP reads PCM from my memory through apps-SMMU stream **0x1b21** (DT qcom,msm-audio-ion iommus <&apps_smmu 0x1b21>, smmu-sid-mask 0xf: Linux hands the DSP addresses with the SID's low bits (1) at bit 32).
    - ADM open (no processing) to port 0x1006 + ASM stream open (16/32-bit PCM), shared-memory map, write buffers; vayu plays 1 kHz at -30 dBFS first, then feeds what iOS writes.
    - **Ours already has `sweet_smmu_identity(sid)` / `sysctl kern.sweetsmmu_map=SID` (0138, USB):** a stage-1 context mapping 0x80000000-0xffffffff to itself. It needs extending with the bit-32 alias (0x1_80000000 -> 0x80000000).
    - Still missing from userland: a physically contiguous buffer under 4 GiB + its PA + a way to fill it. That's a small kernel patch (sysctl: allocate, report PA, write PCM). After it, ASM/ADM can be driven from sweetaudio.py over kern.sweetapr.
- **2026-11-11: 0194 + play-tone: the DSP plays from my memory (CI 43142259986, kernel 2d875b1e, `shim/out/shim-boot-0194-7g-bt-ddr.img`, the 0192 line).**
  - `kern.sweetaudiobuf`: **kmem_alloc_contig below 4 GiB worked** (pa 0xc5d04000; RAM from 0xc0000000 since the 7 GB ADT), uncached map ok. Stream 0x1b21 -> SMR[92] -> bank 54 (identity + the bit-32 alias), DSP address 0x1c5d04000.
  - Bring-up on that boot:

    2. `i2c-init`, `tfa-init`, `mi2s-pins`, `afe-start 2 32`, `tfa-up`, `tfa-mute`.

    - **148 periods (3.0 s), 0 errors, 0 underflows.** The DSP read my buffer through the SMMU; the 16-bit COPP feeding the 32-bit port was accepted.

### >>> 2026-11-10: kernel HID for BT controllers - findings (no patch written) <<<
- **Who consumes game controllers (observed on the phone):** `/usr/libexec/gamecontrollerd`, launchd `com.apple.GameController.gamecontrollerd`, started by LaunchEvents `com.apple.iokit.matching` on **IOProviderClass IOHIDDevice + DeviceUsagePairs GenericDesktop Joystick (1/4) or GamePad (1/5)**. `/System/Library/Extensions/AppleGameControllerPersonality.kext` (39 personalities) only adds IOHIDEventDummyService / IOHIDProviderPropertyMerger entries for known pads (DualSense, Switch, Luna, ...) plus a generic AppleGCHIDEventDummyService on IOHIDInterface. `IOHIDFamily.kext/PlugIns` exists on the phone (IOHIDLib plugin path).
- **What the kernel lacks:** IOHIDResource (+ IOHIDResourceDeviceUserClient), IOHIDUserDevice, IOHIDDevice, IOHIDInterface, IOHIDLibUserClient. 0058 only has my own minimal IOHIDEventService (SweetHID.cpp) + Apple's IOHIDEventServiceUserClient/Queue/IOHIDEvent.
- **Source:** IOHIDFamily-1915.140.3 downloaded to `research/iohid-1915` (gitignored).
  - Resource user client selectors: 0 Create (1 struct: serialized properties), 1 Terminate, 2 HandleReport (1 struct, async), 3 PostReportResponse (2 scalars + struct), 4 RegisterService, 5 ReleaseToken (1 scalar).
  - Embedded personalities (Info-IOHIDFamily-Embedded.plist): IOHIDResource on IOResources (IOResourceMatch IOBSD, IOUserClientClass IOHIDResourceDeviceUserClient); IOHIDProviderPropertyMerger on IOHIDDevice merging IOUserClientClass IOHIDLibUserClient + IOCFPlugInTypes (40A57A4E.., 7DDEECA8.., FA12FA38.. -> IOHIDFamily.kext/PlugIns/IOHIDLib.plugin).
  - Port size: about 17k lines of IOHIDFamily C++ (the classes above plus IOHIDElementPrivate/Container, IOHIDDeviceElementContainer, IOHIDReportElementQueue, IOHIDEventQueue, IOHIDEventSource, IOHIDProviderPropertyMerger, IOHIDFamilyPrivate) + 38 C files of the descriptor parser (IOHIDSystem/IOHIDDescriptorParser, about 7k lines).
  - Known edits needed: IOHIDDevice.cpp handleOpen/handleClose use IOHIKeyboard/IOHIPointing (macOS legacy) for seize messages; IOHIDFamilyPrivate.cpp's IOHIDSystemActivityTickle uses IOHIDSystem::instance(), and handle_stackshot_keychord needs kern_stack_snapshot_with_reason / sysdiagnose_notify_user.
  - Base files for a patch: iokit/conf/files.arm64 and iokit/KernelConfigTables.cpp as of xnu-8796.141.3 + patches; all 13 patches touching them replay cleanly. Repo patches are LF.

### >>> 2026-11-10: show up over USB as a real iPhone (iTunes / 3uTools via usbmux) <<<
- **Goal (seen by hand):** the PC's Apple Mobile Device Service / iTunes / 3uTools connect to the phone like to a real iPhone. FairPlay/SEP out of scope. Replaces the AWDL work (that section is below, paused).
- **lockdownd on the phone (observed, 0178-7g-bthook boot):** runs (`/usr/libexec/lockdownd`, launchd `com.apple.mobile.lockdown`, Sockets TCP 62078 IPv4+IPv6 and unix `/var/run/lockdown.sock` mode 0777). No pair records yet (`/var/root/Library/Lockdown` has only data_ark.plist; `/private/var/db/lockdown` empty). Nothing on the phone does usbmux (no usbmuxd job; on a real iPhone it is the kext AppleUSBDeviceMux).
- **lockdownd only talks to kernel sockets (static, the phone's lockdownd, copy `research/usbmux/lockdownd`, disassembly `lockdownd.s`).** `socket_accept_callback` 0x1000223ac..0x100022578 sorts each accepted connection:
  - **AF_UNIX:** getsockopt(SOL_LOCAL, LOCAL_PEERPID) -> kind 4.
  - **AF_INET 127.0.0.1 / AF_INET6 ::1:** `getsockopt(fd, IPPROTO_TCP, 0x204 = TCP_PEER_PID)` must return **0 = the peer is a kernel socket** -> kind 1 (USB). Anything else logs `peer_is_safe` and closes. This is how it recognises AppleUSBDeviceMux.
  - **Other addresses (Wi-Fi):** kind 3, closed unless one of three settings allows network connections (Wi-Fi sync for a paired host, presumably).
- **Tested (`tools/ldclient.py`, plist protocol = 4-byte BE length + XML plist):** QueryType from 127.0.0.1 on the phone and from the PC to 10.0.0.237:62078 are both closed at once (oslog: `lockdownd[74]: peer_is_safe`). Over the unix socket: `parse_message` error with XML and no answer with a binary plist (framing there differs; not pursued, it is not the USB path).
- **My kernel implements TCP_PEER_PID** (python on the phone: server-side getsockopt(6, 0x204) returned the client's pid 1901). So a mux in the kernel that opens in-kernel TCP sockets (`sock_socket`/`sock_connect` KPI, so->last_pid 0) to 127.0.0.1:62078 should be accepted as USB. Not proven until it runs.
- **Windows driver matching (this PC has Apple Mobile Device Support; Apple Mobile Device Service RUNNING; INFs in DriverStore):**
  - `appleusb.inf`: `USB\VID_05ac&PID_12a8` -> AppleUSB_CCGPDriverInstall = Windows usbccgp (composite parent) + LowerFilters AppleLowerFilter, with **`OriginalConfigurationValue`=2** (usbccgp selects configuration value 2) and UsbccgpCapabilities 0x10.
  - `USB\VID_05ac&PID_12a8&MI_01` -> **AppleUsbMux_Install**: WinUSB + UpperFilters WUDFRd, AppleKmdfFilter; DeviceInterfaceGUIDs `{664be590-54bd-4964-8a8c-6cd1314f6dc2}` (the "New iTunes WinUSB interface"). Also `&RESTORE_MODE&MI_00`.
  - Older `usbaapl64.inf` lists the same PIDs (0x1290-0x12ac, 0x12af) for the whole device; appleusb.inf's FeatureScore 0x7F makes it the newer choice (which one wins on this PC: not checked).
  - So the device needs: VID 0x05ac, PID 0x12a8 (the PID all iPhones from the 5 on use; that part is from memory), a configuration with bConfigurationValue 2 whose **interface 1** is the usbmux interface (class 0xff, subclass 0xfe, protocol 2, one bulk IN + one bulk OUT). Interface 0 on a real iPhone is PTP (still image 6/1/1).
- **My gadget (`bsd/dev/sweet_usb.c`, rebuilt from its 17 patches 0072..0155 in the scratchpad):** DWC3 device mode, one DMA area, ep0 state machine, ECM (sweetusb=2, 1209:0001) or RNDIS (sweetusb=3, 1209:0002, IAD 0xe0/1/3) on bulk 0x01/0x81 (physical eps 2/3) + interrupt 0x82 (5), one TRB per endpoint, ue0 192.168.7.2.
- **Plan (patch 0180+, boot-arg e.g. sweetusb=4):** device descriptor 05ac:12a8; configuration 1 = RNDIS as now (fallback); configuration 2 = interface 0 placeholder (PTP or a vendor interface), **interface 1 usbmux** (bulk OUT 0x04 / IN 0x85, physical eps 8/11 or any free pair), interfaces 2-3 RNDIS inside its IAD (usbccgp splits it, so SSH over USB stays). In the kernel: usbmux device side as in libimobiledevice/usbmuxd (VERSION handshake, 28-byte v2 mux header + TCP header, SYN -> connect an in-kernel socket to 127.0.0.1:dport -> SYN|ACK or RST, data with seq/ack/window, FIN/RST), one kernel thread per connection or a small poller. Unknown: which vendor control requests AppleLowerFilter sends (answer harmlessly, log them).

### >>> 2026-11-10: AWDL (AirDrop) - collecting Apple's pieces for my own awdl0 <<<
- **Goal (seen by hand):** reverse the files Apple's awdl0 uses and write my own kernel patch that creates awdl0 on the 7T (QCA WCN3990 Wi-Fi, redwood_net/topaz). Interop only.
- **Observed on the phone (0178-7g-bthook boot):** no awdl0. Interfaces: lo0 gif0 stf0 utun0 en0 utun1 utun2 ue0. AirDrop is already set to Everyone (`com.apple.sharingd.plist` DiscoverableMode=Everyone, EveryoneModeUpdateTime 2026-11-10 11:10). sharingd and rapportd run, and the AirDrop extension checks in. Transfers cannot work without awdl0. Whether BT LE discovery advertising works was NOT checked.
- **Userland, on the phone:** `/usr/libexec/wifip2pd` (the AWDL daemon; launchd `com.apple.wifip2pd`, on demand, MachServices com.apple.wifip2pd, not running). Frameworks in the dyld shared cache: WiFiPeerToPeer, IO80211, CoreWiFi, MobileWiFi, Sharing, Rapport.
  - **Copied to `research/awdl/wifip2pd`** (gitignored; 4350032 bytes, sha1 9b94cb16c88fb12916bb4d145e510ad07fa254a1, matches the phone).
  - Large copies over USB RNDIS stall: scp stuck at 0 bytes; `ssh cat` got 192 KB in 2 min. Over Wi-Fi (`root@10.0.0.237`) the same copy took seconds.
  - The phone has no `shasum`: use `python3 -c hashlib`.
- **Kernel side:** awdl0 is created by Apple's Wi-Fi kexts (IO80211Family + AppleBCMWLANCore: Skywalk interface, AWDL peer manager, P2P). That code is in the stock kernelcache, which is on neither the phone (nothing under /private/preboot) nor the PC (research/ipsw-x has only d22ap.adt). HackiOS has no AWDL code.
  - Source: the iPhone10,3/10,6 16.5 20F66 IPSW (Apple CDN `updates.cdn-apple.com/2023SpringFCS/fullrestores/032-85130/6AB0D10A-8AF9-4F6C-8CF5-A1B315CABE88/iPhone10,3,iPhone10,6_16.5_20F66_Restore.ipsw`, 6165698198 bytes; URL from api.ipsw.me).
  - Read from the zip's central directory: the only kernelcache is **`kernelcache.release.iphone10b`** (deflate, 18155000 -> 18183395 bytes, local header at offset 6147533282). So it can be fetched alone with HTTP range requests (scratchpad `zipls.py` lists entries this way).
  - **Downloaded (looks right on the phone, 2026-11-10):** only that zip entry, via range requests (scratchpad `zipget.py URL OFFSET CSIZE OUT`) -> `research/awdl/kc/kernelcache.release.iphone10b` (IM4P "krnl", KernelCacheBuilder_release-2429.120.2, sha1 9ac7366d..).
  - Payload is **LZFSE** (bvx2 at byte 63). Decoder on Windows: `pip install lzfse` (binary wheel). Unwrapped -> fat file with one arm64 slice (cpu 0x100000c) -> **`research/awdl/kc/kernelcache.arm64`** (50.9 MB, MH_EXECUTE).
  - Format: the old merged prelinked kernelcache, NOT a fileset (no LC_FILESET_ENTRY). Kexts are listed in `__PRELINK_INFO.__info` (XML with ID/IDREF, so plistlib fails; parse the top-level dicts by depth). 240 bundles, 228 with code. Scratchpad `prelink.py KC NAME...` lists them, dumps the segments of matching kexts (`research/awdl/kc/prelink.txt`) and writes each one's __cstring to `kc/cstr-<bundle>.txt`. For disassembly, load kernelcache.arm64 whole in Ghidra/IDA: both split prelinked kexts themselves.
- **Wi-Fi/AWDL kexts in it (load address):** IOSkywalkFamily 0xfffffff004dd3740, BCMWLANFirmware4357.Hashstore 0xfffffff0050a2b00 (the chip is a BCM4357), **IO80211Family 0xfffffff005409780** (exec size 0x72968), **AppleBCMWLANCore 0xfffffff00547c100**, AppleBCMWLANBusInterfacePCIe 0xfffffff0054fea00.
  - AWDL strings per kext: IO80211Family 793 (p2p 135), AppleBCMWLANCore 292 (p2p 48), BusInterfacePCIe 19, IOSkywalkFamily 0.
  - `research/awdl/apple80211-names.txt`: 585 `APPLE80211_IOC_*` / `APPLE80211_M_*` names; 80 are `APPLE80211_IOC_AWDL_*` (election, sync params/state, channel sequence, peer database, service params, encryption keys, BTLE sync, ...) and 20 are `APPLE80211_M_AWDL*` events. The names are strings; their numbers are not mapped yet (needs the dispatch table in IO80211Family).
  - `research/awdl/awdl-classes.txt` (202 names). The AWDL ones: **IO80211AWDLPeerManager, IO80211AWDLPeer, IO80211AWDLMulticastPeer** (protocol side in IO80211Family), **AppleBCMWLANProximityInterface** (the BCM driver's AWDL interface, guess from the name), AppleBCMWLANLowLatencyInterface (llw), AppleBCMWLANNANInterface/NANDataInterface, AppleBCMWLANSkywalkInterface + Skywalk queues/pools, IO80211InterfaceAwdlMacAddressInUse.
  - IO80211Family also names the AWDL frame TLVs (AWDL_SYNCHRONIZATON_PARAMETERS_TLV, ELECTION_PARAMETERS(_VER2), SERVICE_REQUEST/RESPONSE, CHAN_SEQ, SYNC_TREE_PATH, VERSION, DATA_PATH_STATE, ARPA, NAN_SYNC, DFSP_*). Compare with OWL's list.
- **What wifip2pd needs from the driver (strings, static):** Swift CoreP2P layer (`IO80211Driver`, `IO80211CommandSender`, `IO80211Communicator`). Through IO80211.framework it opens IOKit user clients **`IO80211P2PDaemonUserClient`, `IO80211APIUserClient`, `IO80211AsyncEventUserClient`** (`_Apple80211BindToInterfaceWithService`). It reads `apple80211_capability_data` (loadCapabilities), plus AWDL statistics / low-latency statistics and APPLE80211KEY_AWDL_* stats keys. So awdl0 needs, besides the ifnet, those user clients (or whatever the framework falls back to) and AWDL capability bits. My kernel has no IO80211Family: en0 is my own redwood_net/topaz stand-in.
- **QCA Wi-Fi firmware vs AWDL (2026-11-10, static strings of `wifi/wlanmdsp-a.mbn` = modem_a's WLAN firmware, ELF, 4079408 bytes; the full partition dump is `~/Downloads/UB-Touch/backup/modem_a.img`; `backup/` only has the file list wifi-files.txt). Grouped strings saved in `research/awdl/wlanfw-strings.txt`.**
  - Version `WLAN.HL.3.2.0.c2-00006-QCAHLSWMTPLZ-1.221848.1.222054.4`. Linux ath10k_snoc drives this family.
  - **No AWDL** (0 hits; expected, AWDL is Apple/Broadcom only).
  - **Present:**
    - **NAN 2.0 with data paths** (wlan_nan.c, wlan_nan_chan_sched.c, NAN20_NDP, NANSCHED_*, NAN cluster SM, NAN PMK). This is Wi-Fi Aware, the standard cousin of AWDL: discovery windows, a channel schedule and peer data links.
    - **P2P** (p2p_schedules.c, GO NoA, p2p_listen_offload).
    - **Off-channel scheduler** (ocs_internal_ch_req_create/start/update, resmgr channel manager, CHAN_SWITCH_REQUEST), the time-slotted channel requests NAN/P2P use.
    - **Management-frame TX from the host over WMI** (wlan_mgmt_tx_send_wmi_cmd_handler, MGMT_TX_WMI_FROM_HOST_COMP).
    - **A public action frame filter** (wal_set_public_action_frame_filter, wlan_config_vendor_oui_action).
    - **Per-vdev TSF** (wal_vdev_get_tsf64, wlan_vdev_tsf_tstamp_action).
    - **A monitor RX path** (wal_rx_setup_monitor_mode, htt_tgt_hif_svc_monitor_enable).
  - **Absent:** no raw/inject/native-wifi TX strings.
  - **Guesses, not tested:**
    - AWDL action frames are public action (category 127, vendor-specific, Apple OUI 00:17:f2), so they can probably be sent with WMI mgmt TX, and received if the public-action filter lets them up. Whether "vendor_oui_action" is a pass-through filter or qcacld's interop "action OUI" list is unknown.
    - Sitting on channel 6/149 (what OWL does) should work with a second vdev or the monitor path. Real AWDL availability-window timing would have to be built on the OCS channel requests or on NAN's scheduler.
    - AWDL data frames (BSSID 00:25:00:ff:94:73, Apple LLC/SNAP header) need a vdev that accepts that BSSID: unknown which vdev type allows it.
  - Next for the firmware side: check what my topaz/ath10k code can already create (vdev types, WMI mgmt TX, monitor) against these, and try receiving AWDL action frames from a real Apple device on channel 149/6 first (AirDrop open on it).
- **Next:** (1) map APPLE80211_IOC_AWDL_* names to numbers and I-client selectors (IO80211Family dispatch, IO80211.framework). (2) Find where IO80211Family creates the awdl0 interface (unit name, functional type IFRTYPE_FUNCTIONAL_WIFI_AWDL) and what it asks the BCM driver/firmware for (AWDL timing/availability windows run in the Broadcom firmware). (3) Check whether the QCA WCN3990 firmware can do the equivalent (channel hopping, action-frame TX/RX on a second vif); otherwise run the protocol in software like OWL. Frame format: seemoo-lab OWL and their papers.

### >>> 2026-11-09 (latest): performance back at 60 fps (two settings the 11-08 image had lost) <<<
- **Measured** (tools/op7t-test.sh fps 25, synthetic home-screen swipes): 11-08 image as booted: median **52.5 fps**, sampled frame 11.0 ms, backboardd 93 % CPU, SpringBoard 42 %.
- **Cause 1: `kern.sweetcores` was 255** (the slow A55 cpu0 used again). The 11-08 image (`shim/out/shim-boot.img`, Nov 8) was built **without a boot drop**, so the phone kept an old `prep.sh` that never set it. Set live -> median **60.7**, SpringBoard 42 -> 23 %.
- **Cause 2: `sweetscan=1` was missing from the boot line** -> every frame copied 10 MB into the panel by the CPU instead of flipping the display to the surface (0108). New image **`shim/out/shim-boot-1108-scan.img`** = same kernel (xnu/out, Nov 8, through 0161), same ADT (`shim/out/sweet-mac.adt`, sRGB + MACs), same boot line + `sweetscan=1` (574 bytes). Result: frame **11.0 -> 5.5-7.6 ms**, backboardd **93 -> 45-67 %**, median **60.4** (display cap), flips logged (`HOTDOGB SCAN: flip N`).
- **prep.sh:** the phone's `/private/preboot/jb/procursus/prep.sh` now has the `kern.sweetcores=240` line (copied over SSH; old copy in `/var/root/prep.sh.before-cores`). It persists because the current images carry no drop. Note: the small drop built into 1108-scan (`shim/out/prep-cores.drop`, prep.sh only) was **not applied** (no drop lines in dmesg, file unchanged): not investigated.
- **tools/op7t-test.sh `up`**: returns at once when SSH already answers (sweetusb=3 RNDIS = 1209:0002, Windows runs the link, no usbipd needed).
- **Charging status ~1 min late** (plug/unplug): 0153 polled the PM8150B every 30 s (+ iOS delay). **0163**: USBIN_PLUGIN read every second, full update at once when it changes (SOC still every 30 s).
- **Wi-Fi bars grey while joined:** wifid (restarted while logging with oslog) refused to auto-join: `Below minimum RSSI threshold`; every network scanned at **-82..-94 dBm** next to the router; en0 says RSSI -75 for the joined BSS. Conversion is ath10k's (SNR - 95), so the radio hears ~30-40 dB too little. The firmware reports **board id 0xff -> generic `bdwlan.bin`** is loaded, not the 7T's calibration (likely also why 2.4 GHz never answers auth). **0164**: boot-arg `sweetbdf=FILE` (image/ of the modem partition; candidates `bdwlan.b0a`, `bdwlan.b0c`, `4bdwlan.b0a`, `4bdwlan.b0c`, `5bdwlan.b0a`, `5bdwlan.b0c`, and the `0`/`1` variants). Test each: boot, compare scan RSSI of the home AP (oslog of wifid, or the join diag line).
- **0164 tested (kernel 9aa18a29.., CI run 42752501751 on macos-15 with my new xnu.yml; image `shim/out/shim-boot-0164-b0a.img` = 11-08 line + sweetscan=1 + `sweetbdf=bdwlan.b0a`):** Naja2025 scanned at **-63..-67 dBm** (was -82..-94 with bdwlan.bin), ~20 dB better; **auto-joined by itself** (en0 RSSI -63). Other candidates (4bdwlan/5bdwlan b0a/b0c) not tried yet. Plain image without sweetbdf: `shim/out/shim-boot-0164.img`.
- WSL would not start (HCS 0x800705aa) with 0.6 GB RAM free: `.wslconfig` now memory=1GB, swap=0 (old in `.wslconfig.bak-20261110`, was 4GB/2GB). Enough for image builds and tests.
- **Bars still grey on 0164-b0a (seen 2026-11-10) although RSSI answers -63.** Cause (static, from the device's binaries): wifid monitors `APPLE80211_M_WEIGHT_AVG_LQM_UPDATE` = **event 145** (wifid 0x100048564 `mov w1,#0x91; bl Apple80211StartMonitoringEvent`) and the driver never posted it. IO80211.framework (dumped from a process's memory on the phone, `dumpimg.py`; parser at 0x226599d94 in that boot's cache slide) accepts **exactly 16 bytes = int32 rssi, snr, tx_rate, rx_rate**. **0165** posts it every 5 s while joined (first 1 s after link-up; log "apple80211: link quality: rssi .., snr .."), rates fixed 54; topaz_sta.c also refreshes the joined RSSI from the AP table (<=10 s old, every <=2 s). Made without WSL: files rebuilt from 0124+0135 in Git Bash (only patches touching them), syntax-checked against stubs, `git apply --check` on that base. **Built: CI run 42764799788 (commit 69edfbe), kernel sha A9694779.., has the "link quality" string; now `xnu/out/kernel.development.sweet` (0164 kernel kept as `.0164`). Image built WITHOUT WSL: Windows `python tools/mkpayload.py` + scratchpad `repack.py OLD.img PAYLOAD OUT.img` (swaps the ramdisk of a v2 boot.img, recomputes the id; rebuilding 0164-b0a this way was byte-identical to the WSL-built one) -> `shim/out/shim-boot-0165.img` (0164-b0a line + same drop). First `fastboot boot` failed twice: "AdbWriteEndpointSync failed: semaphore timeout (121)" (USB, phone in fastboot). After a replug it booted (2026-11-10 22:09). **Observed:** auto-joined at 122 s, first "link quality: rssi -65, snr 30" 1 s after link-up, then every 5 s, RSSI moving -61..-65 (the AP-table refresh works). **Bars still grey (seen by hand); wifid logged nothing about 145.**
- **0166: event 39 is the one wifid's LQM handler reads.** wifid 0x100058f80 ("Too frequent(%f secs) LQM event from driver") reads RSSI/SNR/NOISE/CCA/TX*/RX* keys; IO80211's event switch sends code 0x27 to the parser at 0x226599120 (this boot's slide), needs >=180 bytes: 0x00 u8 rssi_valid, 0x04 i32 rssi, 0x08 u8 per-core valid + 2 i8, 0x0b u8 snr_valid, 0x0c i16 snr, 0x0e u8 noise_valid, 0x10 i16 noise, 0x12 u8 cca_valid, 0x13 u8 cca, then counters. (Other switch tables decoded: codes 0x89-0xa5, 0x37-0x5d; 0x91 -> the 16-byte WEIGHT_AVG parser.) 0166 posts 39 next to 145. CI run 42767474037 (commit 1f58827), kernel c10134af, image `shim/out/shim-boot-0166.img`. **WORKS (2026-11-10 22:26): bars show (seen by hand); wifid logs `__WiFiLQAMgrLogStats(Naja2025): Rssi: -63 ... Snr: 32` and `linkQualityDidChange: rssi:-63dBm`.** Remaining wifid complaints: "perCoreRssiHistory is Empty!", LQM_SUMMARY (386) refused 102 (harmless so far).
- **0167** (checked: "more instant"): first LQM at link-up itself, next +1 s, then every 2 s (was +1 s / 5 s); per-core RSSI filled. CI run 42768615476 (commit 5bef036), kernel 2b9d3340, image `shim/out/shim-boot-0167.img` (current recommended: 0164-b0a line + sweetbdf=bdwlan.b0a). **Booted 22:36: wifid now logs every 2 s, `Rssi: -65 {-65 -65}` (per-core filled, "Empty!" gone).** Still refused: LQM_SUMMARY (386, err 102), harmless.

### >>> 2026-11-10: Bluetooth bring-up, step 1 (ADT) <<<
- **Hardware (live FDT `dtb/hotdogb-live.dts`):** `bt_wcn3990` (compatible qca,wcn3990) = the WCN3990's BT. Supplies: vdd-core = pm8150_l7 1.8 V, vdd-pa = pm8150l_l2 1.304 V, vdd-ldo = pm8150l_l11 3.3 V (0x328980). HCI UART = `qcom,qup_uart@0xc8c000` (msm-geni-serial-hs, status ok, wakeup-byte 0xfd, irq via interrupts-extended GIC 0x249). `bt_en_active` pinctrl = gpio172. Firmware (not located yet): QCA "cherokee" files, normally crbtfw21.tlv + crnv21.bin (bluetooth partition / vendor firmware) - verify.
- **Before:** bluetoothd restarted every 10 s: "marconi-bt: Unable to match bluetooth service in device tree", MAC all 00s.
- **How bluetoothd looks:** IOServiceNameMatching("bluetooth") -> IOServiceGetMatchingService -> IORegistryEntrySearchCFProperty(.., "IODeviceTree", "local-mac-address", recursive) (bluetoothd 0x100027238..0x100027288). Then it opens `/dev/uart.bluetooth` (or cu.bluetooth; "No valid serial port found (tried cu.bluetooth and uart.bluetooth)"); BT_HCI_TRANSPORT env / plist picks the transport.
- **Done:** `tools/mkadt.py bluetooth_node()`: `/arm-io/bluetooth` (D22AP's /arm-io/uart1/bluetooth props: compatible bluetooth,n88, local-mac-address 02:54:4f:50:41:5b, transport-speed 3000000, transport-encoding 3, vendor 0x5ac, product 0x12a0, supported-profiles 0x2ffb, coex 2), placed directly under arm-io because only arm-io's children become IOService nubs here (under uart1 it stayed DT-only). For testing it was grafted into the existing ADT: scratchpad `graftbt.py shim/out/sweet-mac.adt shim/out/sweet-bt.adt` -> image `shim/out/shim-boot-0167-bt.img` (0167 + this ADT).
- **After (booted 22:50):** `ioreg` shows `bluetooth <IOPlatformDevice>`; bluetoothd stays up, says "Booting chipset" and runs **BlueTool** (`BlueTool -R`, XPC script com.apple.bluetooth.bluetool.script). BlueTool: "failed to fetch wifi vendor (no AppleOLYHAL node)", Manufacturer -1, chip (null), no override script, "NO_FW_FOUND", "No WiFi - Iteration N", then "BlueTool timed out!" and bluetoothd retries "Booting chipset" in a loop.
- **BlueTool override:** it first looks for `/etc/bluetool/<HWModel>.boot.script` then `/etc/bluetool/<platform>.boot.script` ("HW Model there is override script file %s"), else its built-in Broadcom scripts (firmware in /usr/share/firmware/bluetooth/, .hcd). So a D22AP.boot.script can replace the Broadcom firmware upload.
- **Plan:** (1) kernel: power the WCN3990 BT (3 LDOs), drive the GENI UART at c8c000, QCA firmware download (TLV patch + NVM) in the kernel, and expose `/dev/uart.bluetooth` as an H4 tty with a ready controller; (2) `/etc/bluetool/D22AP.boot.script` that only opens the port / does HCI reset (syntax: read BlueTool's built-in scripts); (3) see which Broadcom/Apple vendor HCI commands bluetoothd then sends and answer or fake them. Check first whether the phone's Android partitions (backups) have the BT firmware.

Pins 43-46 are on the **EAST** TLMM tile (0x03500000; Linux pinctrl-sm8150 PINGROUP(43, EAST, qup13, ..)), not SOUTH; function 1 = qup13. Clock offsets checked against Linux gcc-sm8150 (wrap2 s3: RCG 0x1e4d8, branch 0x1e4d4, vote 0x52014 bit 7). Result:
  - gpio43/44/46 cfg 0x1 (func 0 gpio, pull-down), gpio45 cfg 0x3 (gpio, pull-up): the DT "default"/sleep state; **not muxed to qup13 yet**. Active state wanted: func 1, 43/46 no pull (cfg 0x4), 44 pull-down (0x5), 45 pull-up (0x7), 2 mA.
  - Vote was 0x16 (S0 + AHBs); after setting bit 7: 0x96, **s3 branch 0x4220 = running**.
  - s3 RCG: cfg 0x100000 (src 0 = XO 19.2 MHz, no divider), **DFS off** (dfsr 0x8020, bit 0 clear; all perf levels 0), unlike s0 (dfsr 0x21, DFS table e.g. level 1 = GPLL0_EVEN m 8 n ~0xffbc -> 32 MHz). So SE3 runs on 19.2 MHz unless its RCG is programmed (M/N: N reg = ~(n-m), D = ~n, cfg src 6 = GPLL0_OUT_EVEN, mode 2<<12, CMD bit0 update; 7.3728 MHz = m 384 n 15625, 96 MHz = m 8 n 25).
  - **SE firmware loaded: rev 0x20b = protocol 2 (UART), version 11**; status 0 (idle), output/cgc 0x7f, m_clk 0x11 (enabled, div 1), clk_sel 0, FIFO mode (dma 0), hw params 0x20102864/0x20204900.
  - Baud with 19.2 MHz and 32x oversampling: 600000/div -> 2400 exact (div 250; the WCN3990 power-on pulse), 115200 not exact. Next: 0169 = program the RCG to 7.3728 MHz (115200 = div 2, 2400 = div 96), mux the pins, GENI UART init (FIFO, 8N1, RTS/CTS) after Linux qcom_geni_serial.c, power pulse 0xFC at 2400, then 115200 and the QCA "get version" vendor command + HCI_Reset, logging the replies.

    - H4/POSIX open = 0x10006669c. It takes "name,speed" from env **`BT_HCI_TRANSPORT=<transport>:<driver>:<device>`** (getenv at 0x100155fec, `:` delimiter; transports USB H4DS H4BC H4 H5 APPLEBT VIRT PCIE PCIESW; drivers USB POSIX APPLEBT VIRT PCIE PCIESW). Defaults: com.apple.uart.sk.bluetooth at 2400000.
    - strcmp(name, "com.apple.uart.sk.bluetooth") == 0 -> **Skywalk path**: setsockopt `0x800c5414` ('T' 20) {1024, 63, 63}, then getsockopt `0x40105412` ('T' 18, 16 bytes) = **nexus UUID** -> `uuid_unparse_upper`, `os_channel_create(uuid, 0)`; data goes over a Skywalk channel. Failure return 0x4b1 = the "reason 1201" abort.
    - **Any other name -> termios path**: TIOCGETA, cfmakeraw, cfsetspeed, TIOCSETA, then a plain H4 byte stream on the socket (what 0178's kctl serves).
  - **bthook now (all userland, `tools/bthook/bthook.c`; build: clang `--target=arm64-apple-ios16.0` + `ld64.lld -dylib -install_name /redwood/b ... libSystem.tbd`; then on the phone `ldid -S /var/jb/usr/b`):**
    1. Its constructor sets `BT_HCI_TRANSPORT=H4:POSIX:com.sweet.bt,115200`.
    2. It rewrites the CTLIOCGINFO lookup "com.sweet.bt" -> "com.apple.uart.sk.bluetooth" (kernel id 12). bluetoothd's own strcmp sees the other name and takes the stream path.
    3. It logs every H4 read/write on that socket (first 32 bytes, 400 lines at most).
    4. An H4 receive parser rewrites two kinds of answer:
       - Read Local Supported Commands (0x1002): set octet 18 bit 3 (0x0C5B). Without it bluetoothd quits: "OI_HCICMD_WriteDefaultErroneousDataReporting (status=628)".
       - Command Complete 0x0C5B: status -> 0.
       - **Every Apple/Broadcom vendor command** (OGF 0x3F: 0xFCxx/0xFDxx; the Qualcomm chip answers status 1 with 4-byte params): the event grows by 32 bytes, status 0, then the command's first parameter byte, then zeros.
       - Why the padding: bcm_vsc.c reply parsers assert "ByteStream_NumReadBytesAvail >= N". A short reply means an assert (bcm_vsc.c:642, :139) or a 15 s "BD_VSC_... timedout waiting for controller" -> "Notifying BT Firmware Crashed" -> exit.

BTLEServer's (and bluetoothd's Classic HID) IOHIDUserDeviceCreate opens IOHIDResource, so no HID device can be made. My touch and buttons only work because they are custom kernel services (SweetHIDTouch/SweetHIDButtons, VendorID 1452).

Then Settings **froze** instead; Photos storage plugin also moved aside (assetsd crash-loops: error 41017 "No SSB on file") - did not help. **spindump** (on the phone: `spindump PID 3 -file F`) showed the real hang: Preferences main thread UsagePlugin -> StorageData -> AirTrafficDevice -> FairPlay client lib (non-cache image 2345AE1E-C74C-3C7A-BE81-D8ED08C32733) in mach_msg, "blocked by turnstile ... waiting for launchd send port com.apple.fairplayd.versioned, mach_msg send on intransit port"; atc stuck the same way. **fairplayd.H2 exits (code 0) right after its container lookup; launchd had spawned it 51 times.** With `launchctl bootout system/com.apple.fairplayd.H2` (this boot only) + killing Preferences/atc: **iPhone Storage opens** (15.2 of 17 GB used; per-app sizes keep "calculating"). **FairPlay (2026-11-11):** fairplayd.H2 (launchd `com.apple.fairplayd.H2`, KeepAlive, user mobile; MachServices com.apple.fairplayd, com.apple.fairplayd.versioned, com.apple.unfreed = HostSpecialPort 17; binary /usr/sbin/fairplayd.H2, 19 MB, obfuscated) needs IOKit `com_apple_driver_FairPlayIOKitUserClient` (+ entitlement com.apple.private.FairPlayIOKitUserClient.access) = Apple's FairPlayIOKit kext, absent here -> it exits 0 after its container lookup. **Now disabled for good: `launchctl disable system/com.apple.fairplayd.H2`** (launchd's disabled list; undo `launchctl enable system/com.apple.fairplayd.H2`). I handles FairPlay himself later; no help from here on bypassing it.
  - **Plugins moved back** into StoragePlugins/. Causes found: **Photos** = assetsd exits: tccd "Failed to issue Sandbox Extension for kTCCServicePhotos" (kTCCErrorDomain 6) -> 41017/41002 -> "launch failed"; my sandbox stand-in 0050 answers op 5/6 (extension issue/consume) ENOENT. **Podcasts** = PodcastsUI+0x8dda8 `brk #1` after `[[MPMediaLibrary defaultMediaLibrary] databasePath]` == nil (force unwrap); MediaLibrary.sqlitedb exists and medialibraryd runs, so probably the same TCC/extension problem (MediaLibrary grant). **0174** (CI 42784135875): op 5 issues token "sweet;type;class;path" into the 2048-byte buffer, op 6 returns a handle (layouts read from libsystem_sandbox 16.5). **0174 booted (kernel 2f984878, `shim/out/shim-boot-0174-7g.img`, 7 GiB, 2026-11-11 00:46): assetsd stays up (launchd runs = 1, never exited; Spotlight/knowledge-graph work), no "Failed to issue Sandbox Extension" from tccd any more.** **iPhone Storage now works with both plugins back (seen 2026-11-11).** Tools: scratchpad `dumpimg2.py LIB OUT [SEG]` (dump a loaded image's segment by path), `objc.py` (resolve selectors/class refs: dsc slide is the same in every process).
- **0170 GPU stats (same boot, idle at the home screen):** `addresses:` 199 MB/168 buffers (98 s) -> 262/407 -> 386 MB/414 buffers, 35 holes (109 s) -> 386 -> 300 MB/385 buffers, **58 holes** (145 s); 0 KB lost, 0 refused. Frees do happen; holes already near the old 64 limit (where the old code started losing ranges for good). Still to check: a heavy Safari session, then whether "used" keeps climbing (handles never closed) or stays bounded.
- **RAM:** live FDT memory reg = 0x80000000+0x3bb00000, **0xc0000000+0xc0000000 and 0x180000000+0x100000000: one contiguous 7 GB run 0xc0000000..0x280000000**; no fixed reserved-memory above 0xc0000000 (dynamic pools only below 4 GB). mkadt --highmem uses 0x100000000+0xfcc00000 (~3.95 GB). XNU wants one contiguous range -> up to ~6 GB (0x100000000..0x280000000) or ~7 GB from 0xc0000000 look possible; check what the shim/kernel put in 0xc0000000..0x100000000 and the physmap/page-table sizing first. Image recipe without WSL: mkpayload.py on Windows + scratchpad repack.py on shim-boot-0164-b0a.img.

### >>> 2026-11-08: backboardd crash loop = GPU address space exhausted ("RedwoodGPU: no addresses left") <<<
- backboardd crashed twice (13:07:39, 13:08:29) with EXC_BAD_ACCESS in `libOSMesa.8.dylib`, each restart takes SpringBoard down (flashing/freezing UI). Stack: `CA::OGL::GLESContext::update_compressed_image` -> libredwood-gl +0x23654 -> OSMesa. The faulting helpers (0x727118: set flag at +0x18; 0x727128: refcount inc at +0x14) get x0 = NULL from an unchecked 4 MiB allocation (call at ~0x728b8c).
- websurf.txt: `RedwoodGPU: no addresses left for a buffer of 6-12 MiB`, 403 times (log capped at 400), while the kept cache was at 0 MB. Not an IOKit leak (ioreg counters fall).
- Allocator: 768 MiB window (0x10000000-0x3fffffff), first-fit `k1_free[64]`; frees that cannot merge when the list is full go to `k1_lost` and are never reused. `k1_va_fresh` (if the K1a TLB test fails) drops all frees. IOSurfaces wrapped as buffers hold an address range + the surface until their handle is closed.
- Not yet known which of: fragmentation/lost, surface handles never closed, or k1_va_fresh. The `RedwoodGPU: addresses: ...` stats line would say, but it is gated by `gpu_va != 0` and gpu_va is a plain global = 0, so it never prints. Next: patch 0163 with `gpu_va = 1`, boot, read used/live/largest/holes/lost. Details and Apple ID / Bluetooth scoping in the 2026-11-08 notes file (local, not in git).
- Tools: `crashsum.py` (summarize .ips), `mgids.py` (MobileGestalt identity dump) in /var/root on the phone.

### >>> 2026-11-08 (earlier): Safari black page = stale MobileGestalt cache (P3 from the iPhone X image) <<<
- **Symptom:** Safari UI (toolbar) drew fine, the page area stayed black. WebContent was alive and busy (no crash).
- **Root cause:** the MobileGestalt cache `/private/var/containers/Shared/SystemGroup/systemgroup.com.apple.mobilegestaltcache/Library/Caches/com.apple.MobileGestalt.plist` held `CacheExtra` key `LTI8wHvEYKy8zR1IXBW1uQ` = `P3` (stale, from the iPhone X image), even though the ADT already says `artwork-display-gamut = sRGB`. WebKit.GPU believed the display is wide-gamut and asked for 10-bit surfaces. Kernel patch 0093 silently turns those into plain 8-bit single-plane BGRA, so WebKit drew 10-bit-encoded pixels (and the `b3a8` alpha plane no longer exists) into memory the compositor reads as BGRA -> black page.
- **Evidence:** patch **0162** (new, logs WebKit surface creates): 98 WebKit/Safari creates in websurf.txt, 0 failures. MobileSafari (toolbar) asked BGRA and drew fine. WebKit.GPU asked `b3a8` x86 and `w30r` x8 (including the 1125x1500 / 1125x1536 page tiles), all made as fmt 0x42475241, planes 0. Bergamota's own sRGB fix works the same way (WebKit then asks BGRA).
- **Fix:** `mv` the cache to `/var/root/MobileGestalt.plist.bak`, reopen Safari -> page loaded (slow first load = cache regeneration). No reboot was needed for it to render.
- **Verified by hand:** page renders with WebKit feature flags **GPU Process DOM Rendering and Canvas turned back ON** (Settings > Safari > Advanced > Feature Flags), scrolling and the rest work.
- **Ruled out (don't redo):** compositor (booted without libredwood in backboardd, checked via `sysctl kern.bootargs`, still black), `glcopy=1`, WebKit GPU-process DOM/canvas flags, the sRGB ADT value alone (verified in ioreg; not enough because the MG cache overrode it), WebContent crashing.
- **Still to do / check:**
  1. Confirm the regenerated cache has no P3 (on the phone): `python3 -c "import plistlib,glob;f=glob.glob('/private/var/containers/Shared/SystemGroup/*/Library/Caches/com.apple.MobileGestalt.plist');print(f);d=plistlib.load(open(f[0],'rb'));print([(k,v) for k,v in d.get('CacheExtra',{}).items() if v in ('P3','sRGB')])"`. Empty or sRGB = good. P3 again = MG computes it from somewhere else (device-class table?), find the real source.
  2. After a clean reboot with the UDP listener running, WebKit.GPU creates should say asked `0x42475241` (BGRA), not `0x62336138` / `0x77333072`: `grep -E 'HOTDOGB SURF: com.apple.WebKit.GPU create' websurf.txt | tail -20` (on the PC).
  3. **Bake into the root build:** delete that MobileGestalt cache file in the root-image step (`tools/mkiosroot.sh` or the retweak script, whichever one writes the root image). A reflash / re-roll brings back the stale P3 cache. Same whenever the ADT gamut/product fields change.
  4. Keep 0162 (harmless, web creates capped at 400) or drop it: my call.
- **Tools:** `websurf/websurf.sh` (patch/payload/listen/analyze runner) and `websurf/udp-log.ps1` (PowerShell UdpClient on 5140 appending to `websurf.txt` on the PC; the phone's dmesg only holds ~20 s).
- **Backlog from comparing with Bergamota (same xnu-8796 base), suspects only, nothing proven:** my 0046 `externalMethod` default branch zero-fills unhandled IOSurface selectors (9/10/11 values, 20 setPurgeable, 27 bulk attachments) while Bergamota 0121 keeps them; sandbox stand-in 0050 returns ENOENT for ops 4/5/6/0x43 while Bergamota 0116 implements extension tokens. Revisit if surface/extension issues show up elsewhere. Perf: no respawn loops or CPU thieves in `ps aux`, so Bergamota's disabled.plist trick doesn't apply; `sweetgpufreq=6` (675 MHz) and `sweetddr=1` are prepared but not booted, test them in a separate boot.

- **Wi-Fi works with patch 0161 (seen 2026-11-08):** `xnu/patches/0161-op7t-wifi-5ghz-only-ssid.patch` + boot arg `sweetwifi5g=<ssid>`. For that SSID only 5 GHz BSSIDs are considered. Why: the xfinity gateway's 2.4 GHz BSSID (ch 6, -59 dBm) never answered auth (4 tries acked, nothing back, 0159/0160 logs), but its 5 GHz BSSID joined in 3 ms, and a join picks the strongest BSSID of the name, which was the dead 2.4 GHz one. Off unless the boot arg (or `-DSWEET_WIFI_5G_SSID`) is set. This supersedes the older "en0 sent no frames after a join, no DHCP" note in the wifid entry below. Apple ID / App Store sign-in is the next job.

### >>> 2026-11-08 (earlier that day): wifid SIGABRT on join = no Wi-Fi MAC in the ADT; USB shell works <<<
- **USB shell works.** `ssh root@192.168.7.2` (root, rootless jailbreak layout). python3 is `/var/jb/usr/bin/python3` (ctypes works, can call MGCopyAnswer). No clang, no `ifconfig`, no `netstat`; `ioreg` (`/usr/sbin/ioreg`) works. Copy files with `scp file root@192.168.7.2:/var/root/`.
- **wifid crash (25 reports since Oct 24, all the same frame): uncaught exception from `-[__NSDictionaryM __setObject:forKey:]` on `com.apple.wifid.managerQueue`, on the first join.** Root-caused by disassembling the device's `/usr/sbin/wifid` (llvm-objdump does support arm64; the 4 frames, as image offsets, are 0x8f324, 0xed384, 0x12cbf0, 0x13103c):
  - 0x13103c = `-[WiFiXPCManager XPCManager:XPCConnection:receivedXPCRequest:]` (block), 0x12cbf0 = `-[WiFiXPCManager __associate:XPCConnection:]` (calls the worker at 0xec7f8 with line number 0x3f0), 0xed384 = that worker, 0x8f324 = return address of `CFDictionarySetValue(dict, @"PRIVATE_MAC_ADDRESS_VALUE", mgr->0x1e0)` at 0x8f320 (WFMacRandomisation, `__WiFiManagerWillAssociateCallback` / `__WiFiManagerUpdatePrivateMac`).
  - `mgr->0x1e0` is set at WFMacRandomisation init (0x5f010): `MGCopyAnswer(@"WifiAddressData")`. If that is NULL wifid skips the "INIT : device mac HW address read" log, keeps going, and later inserts NULL into the dict, which raises. Static analysis only; the exception text was never captured.
  - **Confirmed on the phone:** `mgprobe.py` (calls `MGCopyAnswer`) returned `None` for `WifiAddressData`, `WifiAddress` and `BluetoothAddress`. `ioreg -p IODeviceTree` showed no mac property anywhere, and `ioreg -l` (IOService plane) only had `IOMACAddress = <02544f50415a>` on `RedwoodWLANController`, which MobileGestalt does not read.
  - The earlier empty-SSID theory (scan has 46 hidden-network entries with empty SSID) was wrong: an empty NSString is a legal dict key. Patch **0156** (empty-SSID guards) is NOT the fix and is not needed for this crash; it was never built or tested.
- **Fix = ADT only, no kernel rebuild:** `tools/mkadt.py` now adds to `/chosen` `mac-address-wifi0` = `02:54:4f:50:41:5a` (the MAC redwood_net.c gives en0) and `mac-address-bluetooth0` = `02:54:4f:50:41:5b`. Regenerate with `python3 tools/mkadt.py shim/out/sweet-mac.adt --highmem` (run from the repo root: the /product node needs `research/ipsw-x/d22ap.adt`; a "d22ap.adt missing" warning means do not boot that ADT), then `mkpayload.py` + `build.sh` + `fastboot boot` as in the 10-17 status notes. Use the current boot args (cpus=8 since 0078), not the 0057u ones.
- **Verified on the phone after booting the new ADT:** `mgprobe.py` now prints `WifiAddressData CFData 6 bytes 02:54:4f:50:41:5a`, `WifiAddress 02:54:4f:50:41:5a`, `BluetoothAddress 02:54:4f:50:41:5b`. So the property name `mac-address-wifi0` is the one MobileGestalt reads (checked by the probe, not by disassembling libMobileGestalt).
- **NOT yet verified: that a join no longer kills wifid.** Test with wifid running (not frozen): join, then `pgrep wifid` before/after and look for new `wifid-*.ips`. With a real MAC wifid will now run its private-MAC logic and may ask the kernel for request types that are still unanswered (COUNTRY_CODE 51, LOCALE 28, ASSOCIATION_STATUS 50 return EOPNOTSUPP); if it crashes again, map the new offsets the same way (parse ObjC metadata + `llvm-objdump --macho -d`).
- **Tools:** `a80211probe.py` (state | scan | join SSID PASS; talks to en0's Apple80211 ioctls without wifid, `killall -STOP wifid` first) and `mgprobe.py` (asks MobileGestalt for WifiAddressData / WifiAddress / BluetoothAddress) live in `/var/root` on the phone.
- **Still open, separate:** en0 sent no frames after a join, so no DHCP (see 0127).

QEMU smoke test: `python3 tools/mkpayload.py $K research/q0057-hi.adt $S/csq/payloadX.bin --ramdisk $S/csq/root-fork.img --cmdline "debug=0xc serial=3 cpus=1 keepsyms=1 -enable_kprintf_spam -noprogress rd=md0"` + `PATH=~/swift/usr/bin:$PATH python3 tools/qemu-sweet.py ... --secs 40 --kernel $K --fail-on-panic` (S = scratchpad; root-fork.img lives in a temporary scratch dir (csq/) and may be gone in a new session).

**Kernel workflow:** edit `research/bt28/xnu/...` (keep the pre-edit copy in `research/preNNNN/`), syntax check `python3 tools/xnu-cc.py research/run28.log research/bt28 <file>`, make `xnu/patches/NNNN-*.patch` with `diff -u pre bt28 | sed` path rewrite, add new/changed files to `research/check-patch-order.py` list, run it (must say applied + MATCH), commit + push -> GitHub Actions `xnu` builds (~8 min) -> `gh run download <id> -R dipa-studio/ios-sweet -n xnu-kernel -D xnu/out-NNNN`. **Repo is PUBLIC since 2026-10-17** (private macOS runner minutes ran out: "recent account payments have failed"); public = free runners.

**Phone workflow (buttons by hand, commands over ssh):**
- Boot test kernel: phone in fastboot -> `fastboot boot shim/out/shim-boot-X.img`. NEVER `fastboot getvar`.
- **Read logs WITHOUT filming:** after a test I holds Power+VolDown -> fastboot -> `fastboot boot recovery/recovery.img` (OrangeFox, temp) -> wait for `adb shell true` -> `tools/sweet-log-pull.sh /run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/logs/<name>.txt` (reads the 4 MiB console ring the kernel keeps in the last 1025 blocks of userdata; patch 0047). Then `adb reboot bootloader` brings the phone back to fastboot without I.
- **Flash a root image:** `fastboot flash -S 512M userdata <img>.simg` (7 min, 25 chunks). After a big flash the Xiaomi ABL fastboot hangs: next `fastboot boot` stalls at "Sending" -> re-enter fastboot by hand (Power+VolDown). Do not retry commands against the hung ABL. A flash right after `adb reboot bootloader` once failed on chunk 1 (USB protocol error): just retry. Do NOT unplug during flash (once interrupted at 25/25 -> reflash).
- **Edit the phone root without reflashing (same-length only):** find the file's raw offset in the local image (it is one extent; search the image for its first 4 KiB), compare with `adb exec-out dd if=/dev/block/sda34 ...`, then `adb push` changed 4 KiB blocks and `dd of=/dev/block/sda34 seek=... conv=notrunc,fsync`, read back. Recovery kernel has no hfsplus.
- **Pull the whole phone root** (12 min): `adb exec-out "dd if=/dev/block/sda34 bs=1048576 count=16384" > img`; mount ro with udisksctl or `sudo mount -t hfsplus -o loop,ro`. To write into a pulled image: `sudo fsck.hfsplus -fy` first (volume is never cleanly unmounted -> Linux mounts ro even with -o force), then `sudo mount -t hfsplus -o loop,force`.
- Making images: copy latest img with `cp --sparse=always`, mount rw with force, change, `sync; umount`, `sha256sum`, `img2simg`. Keep only the latest image + one fallback on the SSD (/home is ~98% full; big work files live in `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/`, old kernels/images archived in `.../ios-sweet-work/archive/`).

**Diagnostics built into the kernel (all SWEET-only):** persistent console log (0047, boot-arg sweetlog=0 off; flush every ~1 s); `|name| text` copies of /dev/null writes for processes in `sweetnull` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0048); `IOK name:` IOKit lookup/open trace for `sweetiolog` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0049/0054, 400 lines max); `SWEET Sandbox: proc op N` (0050); `STR ...` heap text dump on SIGABRT/SIGTRAP for `sweetscan` (default backboardd,SpringBoard; 0051/0053) - this is how NSException/BSAssert reasons were found; `SMP name tN pc .. bt ..` user stack sampler for `sweetsample` (default SpringBoard,backboardd; first at 90 s then every 120 s, 40 samples, 16 frames; 0054/0055); `SWEET FB: N swaps` every 1000 swaps; `CRASH name ...` crash reports (0043/0044). Symbolicate cache addresses with `ipsw dyld a2s research/dsc/dyld_shared_cache_arm64 <addr - slide>`; main-binary addresses: addr - mh + 0x100000000.

- **v11 still migrated (boot 0056u, 20:32 UTC, `logs/boot-0056u-v11.txt`).** On iOS kCFPreferencesCurrentHost lands in the plain plist: all DMEnvironment setters (setLastBuildVersionPref, setUserDataDispositionPref, ...) use CurrentHost and the phone's `/var/mobile/Library/Preferences/com.apple.migration.plist` holds DMUserDataDisposition; datamigrator's XPC service runs with `_RoleAccount: mobile`. **Image v12** = v11 + `LastSystemVersion` = 20H392 added to that plain `com.apple.migration.plist` (keeps DMUserDataDisposition, 501:501 0600) + `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/VirtualAudio.plugin` moved to `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL.disabled/` (stops the mediaserverd SIGSEGV loop; untested). **installcoordinationd loop explained (`reports/installcoordinationd-report.md`):** os_crash "Failed to create data directory" EACCES for `/private/var/containers/Shared/SystemGroup/systemgroup.com.apple.installcoordinationd/Library/InstallCoordination`: `Library`/`Library/Caches` in the container are ACL-only (uid/gid 0xfffffffe, mode 0000, ACE for group GUID ...012B = gid 299 systemusers); with `config_ext_resolver` the kernel asks an identity resolver (memberd/opendirectoryd) that iOS does not run -> EWOULDBLOCK -> ACE skipped -> EACCES. **Patch 0057**: drop config_ext_resolver from FILESYS_BASE (compatibility UID/GID<->GUID mapping is used instead), likely fixes other ACL-protected containers too. task 3: UFS driver performance design (`reports/ufs-performance.md`).
- **mediaserverd SIGSEGV loop explained (report `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/reports/mediaserverd-report.md`):** the fault (FAR 0x390) is in the HAL plug-in `/Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/VirtualAudio.plugin/VirtualAudio` (ApplyBeamFormerPreference <- VirtualAudio_PlugIn::SetPropertyData <- HALS_AHPPlugIn::ObjectSetPropertyData <- AudioObjectSetPropertyData <- MediaExperience vaemVADSerializationListener): the default virtual audio device ('vdef', VirtualAudioDevice_Default) does not exist (no audio hardware/driver, no /product/audio). Proposed workaround for GUI bring-up: move VirtualAudio.plugin out of /Library/Audio/Plug-Ins/HAL/ in the next root image (untested). v11 reflashed OK after an interrupted flash (USB unplugged at chunk 25/25). task 2: installcoordinationd crash loop.
- **ROOT CAUSE of the endless migration: no IOPlatformUUID (2026-10-17).** datamigrator's `-[DMMigrator isMigrationNeededWithBuildVersion:lastBuildVersion:clientPID:]` gets lastBuildVersion from `-[DMEnvironment lastBuildVersionPref]` = `CFPreferencesCopyValue("LastSystemVersion", "com.apple.migration", kCFPreferencesCurrentUser, kCFPreferencesCurrentHost)` -> a ByHost file. The phone had `/var/mobile/Library/Preferences/com.apple.migration.plist` (only DMUserDataDisposition) and NO `Preferences/ByHost` directory at all. On arm XNU publishes IOPlatformUUID (gethostuuid) only from `/chosen/unique-chip-id` (8 bytes) + `/chosen/chip-id` (4 bytes) (IOPlatformExpertDevice::generatePlatformUUID, called from IOPlatformExpert::start); the SWEET ADT had neither, so every CurrentHost preference write failed. Fix: mkadt adds `unique-chip-id` = 0x5357454554000001 (synthetic) and `chip-id` = 0x8015 -> UUID `A9683613-3704-53F2-95D0-98A8FB574E78` (same SHA1 namespace computation as the kernel). ADT `shim/out/sweet-0057hi.adt`, boot image `shim/out/shim-boot-0056u.img` (0056 kernel + 0057hi ADT, QEMU OK). **Image v11** = v10 + `Preferences/ByHost/com.apple.migration.<UUID upper and lower>.plist` {LastSystemVersion: 20H392} (501:501, dir 0755 files 0600). Flashing v11 2026-10-17 ~20:10 UTC. Analysing the mediaserverd SIGSEGV loop in `/run/media/dipa/android/ios-sweet-work/reports/` (report `mediaserverd-report.md`).
